## Researcher–agent collaboration



Chronological shared conversation. The researcher sees these handoffs, not private tool output. Full tool and model records follow.

### 1. Researcher — initial_request

Please inspect the staged repository and relevant inputs, especially capital.csv and any analysis scripts/notebooks or documentation that show how the 100-year-bin PCA was conducted. Report the available files and relevant data structure, describe a proposed analysis workflow for answering how the symbolic-capital variables (house size, daggers, hatchets/swords) load on PC1 and are distributed on PC2, and flag consequential assumptions or uncertainties (including PCA orientation/sign and treatment of missing/interpolated values). Do not yet execute the full analysis or provide the final findings.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** initial_inspection; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The brief identifies capital.csv as the PCA dataset and defines the three symbolic-capital indicators and hundred-year bins, but provides no computed PCA results or implementation details. Initial inspection should establish source structure and analysis choices before authorizing the main work.

**Open objectives:** Inspect repository, staged data, and existing PCA implementation/documentation.; Propose a reproducible workflow and identify consequential uncertainties before full analysis.

**Agent reply (plan)**

Repository inspection found only `task.json` and three CSVs under `data/`; there are no staged notebooks, analysis scripts, or separate documentation demonstrating the original 100-year-bin PCA. `capital.csv` has 39 rows and 15 columns: `BCE`, two categorical annotation columns (`group`, `color`), and 12 numeric-looking archaeological indicators. Relevant variables are `Zhausgr` (house size), `ZDolch` (daggers), and `ZAxtSchwert` (hatchets/swords), alongside nine other capital measures. Values use decimal commas in the raw CSV. Rows shown begin at BCE 4300, 4200, etc., so the records appear to be already discretized at 100-year intervals. No fields are missing in this file. The much denser `time_series_data.csv` (3,601 rows) includes corresponding sparse raw series with 3,564 missing values each and fully populated `_inter` counterparts; it may clarify interpolation lineage, but it is not direct evidence of which inputs were used for PCA.

**Shared evidence:**

Commands listed files: `./data/capital.csv`, `./data/time_series_data.csv`, `./data/pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv`, and `./task.json`; no scripts/notebooks/docs were present. Pandas inspection: capital.csv shape (39, 15), no missing entries; columns include BCE/group/color and twelve Z indicators. First rows are 4300, 4200, 4100 BCE. time_series_data.csv shape (3601, 37), with 3,564 missing raw values for each of the archaeological indicator series and no missing values in their corresponding `_inter` series.

### 2. Researcher — next_task

The proposed workflow is appropriate. Proceed with the analysis using capital.csv as the primary input: verify the 100-year row spacing, numeric parsing, completeness, and feature columns, then run a clearly specified PCA excluding BCE and annotation columns. Report the symbolic variables’ PC1 loadings and PC2 loadings, and distinguish these from any PC2 scores of the time bins. State the scaling convention and variance explained, and note the arbitrary PCA sign. Because the original PCA settings are undocumented, treat choices such as covariance versus correlation and any interpolation sensitivity comparison explicitly as assumptions rather than established study procedure; do not replace the complete capital.csv inputs with interpolated time-series values without justification.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** approach_review; **approach approved:** True

**Basis:** The handoff reports no scripts or documentation specifying the PCA, but capital.csv is complete, has 39 rows and includes the named Z variables; rows begin at 100-year intervals. The proposed analysis appropriately identifies preprocessing, PCA convention, sign ambiguity, and loading-versus-score interpretation as issues to resolve.

**Open objectives:** Verify capital.csv parsing, row intervals, and features; Compute and report PCA results for symbolic-capital variables with method and limitations

**Agent reply (result)**

Using the 39 complete hundred-year rows in `capital.csv` and a correlation-matrix PCA of its 12 Z-indicator variables (each column centered and standardized to unit sample variance; `BCE`, `group`, and `color` excluded), house size (`Zhausgr`), daggers (`ZDolch`), and hatchets/swords (`ZAxtSchwert`) all have positive PC1 loadings: +0.429, +0.507, and +0.570, respectively. Thus each aligns positively with PC1 in the reported orientation; daggers and hatchets/swords are somewhat stronger PC1 associations than house size. For PC2 loadings, house size is notably positive (+0.500), while daggers (+0.113) and hatchets/swords (+0.082) are small positive associations, so PC2 differentiates house size more strongly among these three. PC1 explains 30.3% and PC2 19.5% of standardized variance (49.8% cumulative). These are variable loadings (correlations with component scores), not time-slice scores. The separate PC2 scores describe where the time bins lie: they span -3.648 to +2.503; the highest is at 3200 BCE and the lowest at 4100–4300 BCE (the last three are identical in these inputs). Sign orientation is arbitrary; reversing either component reverses its loadings and scores without changing the PCA. These results are a specified reproducible analysis of the supplied capital table, not a claim about undocumented original PCA settings.

**Shared evidence:**

Data diagnostics: shape 39×15; 39 unique BCE rows, range 500–4300, every sorted adjacent difference 100 years; 12 candidate indicator columns with 0 missing and 0 nonfinite values. PCA results: eigenvalues PC1=3.6393, PC2=2.3359; variance shares 30.3278% and 19.4656%. Symbolic loadings: Zhausgr PC1 0.4294/PC2 0.5004; ZDolch PC1 0.5066/PC2 0.1134; ZAxtSchwert PC1 0.5696/PC2 0.0825. PC2 score range is -3.6479 at 4100, 4200, and 4300 BCE (tied) to +2.5030 at 3200 BCE. Scikit-learn was unavailable, so PCA was implemented directly via NumPy eigen-decomposition of the standardized sample correlation matrix.

### 3. Researcher — conclude

The reported PCA provides a clear, data-grounded answer: in the specified correlation PCA, house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords all have positive PC1 loadings; house size has a substantially larger PC2 loading than either weapon variable. These are variable loadings, distinct from the distribution of bin scores on PC2. The method and arbitrary component sign are stated, and the result is appropriately qualified as a reproducible analysis rather than a reconstruction of undocumented original settings. No further revision is needed.

**Decision:** stop

**Review phase:** result_review; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The handoff reports checks of 39 complete 100-year observations and 12 numeric features, along with the PCA convention, variance shares, symbolic-variable loadings on PC1/PC2, and a distinction between variable loadings and time-bin PC2 scores. It also notes the arbitrariness of signs and limitations from undocumented original PCA settings, consistent with the agreed approach.

**Open objectives:** None

{
  "id": "call_G3OAerx8asQPRdyNHSAkLfJk",
  "responses_item_id": null,
  "name": "execute_command",
  "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Verify capital rows and run specified standardized PCA\", \"command\": \"cat > /tmp/pca_capital.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\np='data/capital.csv'\\ndf=pd.read_csv(p, decimal=',')\\nfeatures=[c for c in df.columns if c not in ['BCE','group','color']]\\nX=df[features].apply(pd.to_numeric, errors='coerce')\\nprint('shape',df.shape,'features',len(features))\\nprint('BCE ascending?',df.BCE.is_monotonic_increasing,'unique',df.BCE.nunique(),'diffs',sorted(df.BCE.sort_values().diff().dropna().unique()))\\nprint('BCE endpoints',df.BCE.min(),df.BCE.max(),'missing numeric',X.isna().sum().sum(),'nonfinite',(~np.isfinite(X.to_numpy())).sum())\\nprint('feature columns',features)\\n# Correlation PCA (center and scale each feature to unit sample variance).\\nXs=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\\npca=PCA().fit(Xs)\\n# Components are unit eigenvectors; standardized-variable correlations with scores are loadings.\\nloadings=pca.components_.T*np.sqrt(pca.explained_variance_)\\nprint('variance explained',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:5], 'cum2',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum())\\nfor name in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n j=features.index(name)\\n print(name,'PC1 eigenvector',pca.components_[0,j],'PC1 corr-loading',loadings[j,0],'PC2 eigenvector',pca.components_[1,j],'PC2 corr-loading',loadings[j,1])\\nprint('loadings all PC1/PC2')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(loadings[:,:2],index=features,columns=['PC1','PC2']).round(4).to_string())\\nprint('scores by BCE first/last')\\nsc=pd.DataFrame(pca.transform(Xs)[:,:2],columns=['PC1_score','PC2_score']);sc['BCE']=df.BCE\\nprint(sc[['BCE','PC1_score','PC2_score']].to_string(index=False))\\nPY\\npython /tmp/pca_capital.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 60}",
  "origin": "completion"
}

<details>

````json
{
  "id": "call_Cdyf1QPHJMfv4DEbn6h9fjER",
  "responses_item_id": null,
  "name": "execute_command",
  "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Compute correlation PCA with NumPy eigen decomposition\", \"command\": \"cat > /tmp/pca_capital.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\np='data/capital.csv'\\ndf=pd.read_csv(p, decimal=',')\\nfeatures=[c for c in df.columns if c not in ['BCE','group','color']]\\nX=df[features].apply(pd.to_numeric, errors='coerce')\\nprint('shape',df.shape,'features',len(features))\\nprint('BCE monotonic ascending?',df.BCE.is_monotonic_increasing,'unique',df.BCE.nunique(),'diffs sorted ascending BCE',sorted(df.BCE.sort_values().diff().dropna().unique()))\\nprint('BCE endpoints',df.BCE.min(),df.BCE.max(),'missing numeric',X.isna().sum().sum(),'nonfinite',(~np.isfinite(X.to_numpy())).sum())\\nprint('feature columns',features)\\n# Correlation PCA: subtract feature mean and divide by sample standard deviation.\\nmu=X.mean().to_numpy(); sd=X.std(ddof=1).to_numpy(); Z=(X.to_numpy()-mu)/sd\\nR=Z.T@Z/(len(X)-1)\\nvals, vecs=np.linalg.eigh(R); order=np.argsort(vals)[::-1]; vals=vals[order]; vecs=vecs[:,order]\\n# Fix deterministic sign only for reporting: make largest-absolute PC1 loading positive.\\n# PC2 sign set by largest-absolute entry likewise; this convention is not substantive.\\nfor k in [0,1]:\\n if vecs[np.argmax(np.abs(vecs[:,k])),k]<0: vecs[:,k]*=-1\\nrat=vals/vals.sum(); load=vecs*np.sqrt(vals)\\nscores=Z@vecs\\nprint('eigenvalues first 5',vals[:5]); print('variance explained first 5',rat[:5], 'cum2',rat[:2].sum())\\nfor name in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n j=features.index(name)\\n print(name,'PC1 loading/correlation',load[j,0],'PC2 loading/correlation',load[j,1],'PC1 eigenvector coefficient',vecs[j,0],'PC2 eigenvector coefficient',vecs[j,1])\\nprint('loadings all PC1/PC2')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(load[:,:2],index=features,columns=['PC1','PC2']).round(4).to_string())\\nprint('scores by BCE ascending')\\nsc=pd.DataFrame(scores[:,:2],columns=['PC1_score','PC2_score']);sc['BCE']=df.BCE\\nprint(sc.sort_values('BCE')[['BCE','PC1_score','PC2_score']].round(4).to_string(index=False))\\nprint('PC2 score summary',sc.PC2_score.describe().round(4).to_dict())\\nprint('PC2 score range BCE',sc.loc[sc.PC2_score.idxmin(),'BCE'],sc.loc[sc.PC2_score.idxmax(),'BCE'])\\nPY\\npython /tmp/pca_capital.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 60}",
  "origin": "completion"
}

  "command": "cat > /tmp/pca_capital.py <<'PY'\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\np='data/capital.csv'\ndf=pd.read_csv(p, decimal=',')\nfeatures=[c for c in df.columns if c not in ['BCE','group','color']]\nX=df[features].apply(pd.to_numeric, errors='coerce')\nprint('shape',df.shape,'features',len(features))\nprint('BCE ascending?',df.BCE.is_monotonic_increasing,'unique',df.BCE.nunique(),'diffs',sorted(df.BCE.sort_values().diff().dropna().unique()))\nprint('BCE endpoints',df.BCE.min(),df.BCE.max(),'missing numeric',X.isna().sum().sum(),'nonfinite',(~np.isfinite(X.to_numpy())).sum())\nprint('feature columns',features)\n# Correlation PCA (center and scale each feature to unit sample variance).\nXs=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\npca=PCA().fit(Xs)\n# Components are unit eigenvectors; standardized-variable correlations with scores are loadings.\nloadings=pca.components_.T*np.sqrt(pca.explained_variance_)\nprint('variance explained',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:5], 'cum2',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum())\nfor name in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\n j=features.index(name)\n print(name,'PC1 eigenvector',pca.components_[0,j],'PC1 corr-loading',loadings[j,0],'PC2 eigenvector',pca.components_[1,j],'PC2 corr-loading',loadings[j,1])\nprint('loadings all PC1/PC2')\nprint(pd.DataFrame(loadings[:,:2],index=features,columns=['PC1','PC2']).round(4).to_string())\nprint('scores by BCE first/last')\nsc=pd.DataFrame(pca.transform(Xs)[:,:2],columns=['PC1_score','PC2_score']);sc['BCE']=df.BCE\nprint(sc[['BCE','PC1_score','PC2_score']].to_string(index=False))\nPY\npython /tmp/pca_capital.py",

}

{
  "command": "cat > /tmp/pca_capital.py <<'PY'\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\np='data/capital.csv'\ndf=pd.read_csv(p, decimal=',')\nfeatures=[c for c in df.columns if c not in ['BCE','group','color']]\nX=df[features].apply(pd.to_numeric, errors='coerce')\nprint('shape',df.shape,'features',len(features))\nprint('BCE monotonic ascending?',df.BCE.is_monotonic_increasing,'unique',df.BCE.nunique(),'diffs sorted ascending BCE',sorted(df.BCE.sort_values().diff().dropna().unique()))\nprint('BCE endpoints',df.BCE.min(),df.BCE.max(),'missing numeric',X.isna().sum().sum(),'nonfinite',(~np.isfinite(X.to_numpy())).sum())\nprint('feature columns',features)\n# Correlation PCA: subtract feature mean and divide by sample standard deviation.\nmu=X.mean().to_numpy(); sd=X.std(ddof=1).to_numpy(); Z=(X.to_numpy()-mu)/sd\nR=Z.T@Z/(len(X)-1)\nvals, vecs=np.linalg.eigh(R); order=np.argsort(vals)[::-1]; vals=vals[order]; vecs=vecs[:,order]\n# Fix deterministic sign only for reporting: make largest-absolute PC1 loading positive.\n# PC2 sign set by largest-absolute entry likewise; this convention is not substantive.\nfor k in [0,1]:\n if vecs[np.argmax(np.abs(vecs[:,k])),k]<0: vecs[:,k]*=-1\nrat=vals/vals.sum(); load=vecs*np.sqrt(vals)\nscores=Z@vecs\nprint('eigenvalues first 5',vals[:5]); print('variance explained first 5',rat[:5], 'cum2',rat[:2].sum())\nfor name in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\n j=features.index(name)\n print(name,'PC1 loading/correlation',load[j,0],'PC2 loading/correlation',load[j,1],'PC1 eigenvector coefficient',vecs[j,0],'PC2 eigenvector coefficient',vecs[j,1])\nprint('loadings all PC1/PC2')\nprint(pd.DataFrame(load[:,:2],index=features,columns=['PC1','PC2']).round(4).to_string())\nprint('scores by BCE ascending')\nsc=pd.DataFrame(scores[:,:2],columns=['PC1_score','PC2_score']);sc['BCE']=df.BCE\nprint(sc.sort_values('BCE')[['BCE','PC1_score','PC2_score']].round(4).to_string(index=False))\nprint('PC2 score summary',sc.PC2_score.describe().round(4).to_dict())\nprint('PC2 score range BCE',sc.loc[sc.PC2_score.idxmin(),'BCE'],sc.loc[sc.PC2_score.idxmax(),'BCE'])\nPY\npython /tmp/pca_capital.py",

Correlation PCA: z-score each feature using column mean and sample standard deviation, form the 12x12 sample correlation matrix, eigendecompose and order eigenvalues descending. Variable loadings are eigenvectors multiplied by square roots of eigenvalues (equivalently correlations between standardized feature columns and component scores); time-bin scores are standardized rows projected onto eigenvectors. Sign orientation selected deterministically for reporting and is arbitrary. No interpolation sensitivity analysis performed because capital.csv is complete and original preprocessing/settings are undocumented."

  "answer": "Using the 39 complete hundred-year rows in `capital.csv` and a correlation-matrix PCA of its 12 Z-indicator variables (each column centered and standardized to unit sample variance; `BCE`, `group`, and `color` excluded), house size (`Zhausgr`), daggers (`ZDolch`), and hatchets/swords (`ZAxtSchwert`) all have positive PC1 loadings: +0.429, +0.507, and +0.570, respectively. Thus each aligns positively with PC1 in the reported orientation; daggers and hatchets/swords are somewhat stronger PC1 associations than house size. For PC2 loadings, house size is notably positive (+0.500), while daggers (+0.113) and hatchets/swords (+0.082) are small positive associations, so PC2 differentiates house size more strongly among these three. PC1 explains 30.3% and PC2 19.5% of standardized variance (49.8% cumulative). These are variable loadings (correlations with component scores), not time-slice scores. The separate PC2 scores describe where the time bins lie: they span -3.648 to +2.503; the highest is at 3200 BCE and the lowest at 4100–4300 BCE (the last three are identical in these inputs). Sign orientation is arbitrary; reversing either component reverses its loadings and scores without changing the PCA. <details>

````text
You are a research agent collaborating with a researcher on the supplied benchmark.
Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.
First inspect inputs/repository and propose an approach. Before the main analysis or
implementation, yield a stage=plan handoff: describe observations, proposed choices,
assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

You are a research agent collaborating with a researcher on the supplied benchmark.
Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.
First inspect inputs/repository and propose an approach. Before the main analysis or
implementation, yield a stage=plan handoff: describe observations, proposed choices,
assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

Human impact or landscape openness, respectively, as reflected in the pollen data, can be used as a demographic indicator based on the assumption that an increasing population density leads to increasing woodland clearance due to an increasing demand for resources including wood, agricultural land and settlement areas. Each sample from the pollen record used in the principal component analysis is absolutely dated and therefore the openness score (PC 1.) can be plotted as a time series, expressing human induced land clearance. The 'Belau_PC1' of pollen data (pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv) has been assumed to reflect openness. Original openness score through 'Belau_PC1', 100-year smoothed openness score, and linear interpolation of openness score have been used to signify growth. The original openness score (Belau_PC1), the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_inter_100), and the linear interpolation of the openness score (pollen_inter) have been used to signify openness. 6. Demographic growth manifests itself in growth set bringing with it a further opening of the landscape. Opening of landscape corresponds to higher growth rates. During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape. The growth rate is defined as the percentage change of the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_grate_100). 7.         "name": "execute_command",
        "arguments": "{\"summary\":\"Verify capital rows and run specified standardized PCA\",\"command\":\"cat > /tmp/pca_capital.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\np='data/capital.csv'\\ndf=pd.read_csv(p, decimal=',')\\nfeatures=[c for c in df.columns if c not in ['BCE','group','color']]\\nX=df[features].apply(pd.to_numeric, errors='coerce')\\nprint('shape',df.shape,'features',len(features))\\nprint('BCE ascending?',df.BCE.is_monotonic_increasing,'unique',df.BCE.nunique(),'diffs',sorted(df.BCE.sort_values().diff().dropna().unique()))\\nprint('BCE endpoints',df.BCE.min(),df.BCE.max(),'missing numeric',X.isna().sum().sum(),'nonfinite',(~np.isfinite(X.to_numpy())).sum())\\nprint('feature columns',features)\\n# Correlation PCA (center and scale each feature to unit sample variance).\\nXs=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\\npca=PCA().fit(Xs)\\n# Components are unit eigenvectors; standardized-variable correlations with scores are loadings.\\nloadings=pca.components_.T*np.sqrt(pca.explained_variance_)\\nprint('variance explained',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:5], 'cum2',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum())\\nfor name in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n j=features.index(name)\\n print(name,'PC1 eigenvector',pca.components_[0,j],'PC1 corr-loading',loadings[j,0],'PC2 eigenvector',pca.components_[1,j],'PC2 corr-loading',loadings[j,1])\\nprint('loadings all PC1/PC2')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(loadings[:,:2],index=features,columns=['PC1','PC2']).round(4).to_string())\\nprint('scores by BCE first/last')\\nsc=pd.DataFrame(pca.transform(Xs)[:,:2],columns=['PC1_score','PC2_score']);sc['BCE']=df.BCE\\nprint(sc[['BCE','PC1_score','PC2_score']].to_string(index=False))\\nPY\\npython /tmp/pca_capital.py\",\"timeout_seconds\":60}"
      }

        "arguments": "{\"summary\":\"Compute correlation PCA with NumPy eigen decomposition\",\"command\":\"cat > /tmp/pca_capital.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\np='data/capital.csv'\\ndf=pd.read_csv(p, decimal=',')\\nfeatures=[c for c in df.columns if c not in ['BCE','group','color']]\\nX=df[features].apply(pd.to_numeric, errors='coerce')\\nprint('shape',df.shape,'features',len(features))\\nprint('BCE monotonic ascending?',df.BCE.is_monotonic_increasing,'unique',df.BCE.nunique(),'diffs sorted ascending BCE',sorted(df.BCE.sort_values().diff().dropna().unique()))\\nprint('BCE endpoints',df.BCE.min(),df.BCE.max(),'missing numeric',X.isna().sum().sum(),'nonfinite',(~np.isfinite(X.to_numpy())).sum())\\nprint('feature columns',features)\\n# Correlation PCA: subtract feature mean and divide by sample standard deviation.\\nmu=X.mean().to_numpy(); sd=X.std(ddof=1).to_numpy(); Z=(X.to_numpy()-mu)/sd\\nR=Z.T@Z/(len(X)-1)\\nvals, vecs=np.linalg.eigh(R); order=np.argsort(vals)[::-1]; vals=vals[order]; vecs=vecs[:,order]\\n# Fix deterministic sign only for reporting: make largest-absolute PC1 loading positive.\\n# PC2 sign set by largest-absolute entry likewise; this convention is not substantive.\\nfor k in [0,1]:\\n if vecs[np.argmax(np.abs(vecs[:,k])),k]<0: vecs[:,k]*=-1\\nrat=vals/vals.sum(); load=vecs*np.sqrt(vals)\\nscores=Z@vecs\\nprint('eigenvalues first 5',vals[:5]); print('variance explained first 5',rat[:5], 'cum2',rat[:2].sum())\\nfor name in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n j=features.index(name)\\n print(name,'PC1 loading/correlation',load[j,0],'PC2 loading/correlation',load[j,1],'PC1 eigenvector coefficient',vecs[j,0],'PC2 eigenvector coefficient',vecs[j,1])\\nprint('loadings all PC1/PC2')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(load[:,:2],index=features,columns=['PC1','PC2']).round(4).to_string())\\nprint('scores by BCE ascending')\\nsc=pd.DataFrame(scores[:,:2],columns=['PC1_score','PC2_score']);sc['BCE']=df.BCE\\nprint(sc.sort_values('BCE')[['BCE','PC1_score','PC2_score']].round(4).to_string(index=False))\\nprint('PC2 score summary',sc.PC2_score.describe().round(4).to_dict())\\nprint('PC2 score range BCE',sc.loc[sc.PC2_score.idxmin(),'BCE'],sc.loc[sc.PC2_score.idxmax(),'BCE'])\\nPY\\npython /tmp/pca_capital.py\",\"timeout_seconds\":60}"

        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Verify capital rows and run specified standardized PCA\", \"command\": \"cat > /tmp/pca_capital.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\np='data/capital.csv'\\ndf=pd.read_csv(p, decimal=',')\\nfeatures=[c for c in df.columns if c not in ['BCE','group','color']]\\nX=df[features].apply(pd.to_numeric, errors='coerce')\\nprint('shape',df.shape,'features',len(features))\\nprint('BCE ascending?',df.BCE.is_monotonic_increasing,'unique',df.BCE.nunique(),'diffs',sorted(df.BCE.sort_values().diff().dropna().unique()))\\nprint('BCE endpoints',df.BCE.min(),df.BCE.max(),'missing numeric',X.isna().sum().sum(),'nonfinite',(~np.isfinite(X.to_numpy())).sum())\\nprint('feature columns',features)\\n# Correlation PCA (center and scale each feature to unit sample variance).\\nXs=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\\npca=PCA().fit(Xs)\\n# Components are unit eigenvectors; standardized-variable correlations with scores are loadings.\\nloadings=pca.components_.T*np.sqrt(pca.explained_variance_)\\nprint('variance explained',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:5], 'cum2',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum())\\nfor name in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n j=features.index(name)\\n print(name,'PC1 eigenvector',pca.components_[0,j],'PC1 corr-loading',loadings[j,0],'PC2 eigenvector',pca.components_[1,j],'PC2 corr-loading',loadings[j,1])\\nprint('loadings all PC1/PC2')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(loadings[:,:2],index=features,columns=['PC1','PC2']).round(4).to_string())\\nprint('scores by BCE first/last')\\nsc=pd.DataFrame(pca.transform(Xs)[:,:2],columns=['PC1_score','PC2_score']);sc['BCE']=df.BCE\\nprint(sc[['BCE','PC1_score','PC2_score']].to_string(index=False))\\nPY\\npython /tmp/pca_capital.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 60}"

sd=X.std(ddof=1).to_numpy(); Z=(X.to_numpy()-mu)/sd\\nR=Z.T@Z/(len(X)-1)\\nvals, vecs=np.linalg.eigh(R); order=np.argsort(vals)[::-1]; vals=vals[order]; vecs=vecs[:,order]\\n# Fix deterministic sign only for reporting: make largest-absolute PC1 loading positive.\\n# PC2 sign set by largest-absolute entry likewise; this convention is not substantive.\\nfor k in [0,1]:\\n if vecs[np.argmax(np.abs(vecs[:,k])),k]<0: vecs[:,k]*=-1\\nrat=vals/vals.sum(); load=vecs*np.sqrt(vals)\\nscores=Z@vecs\\nprint('eigenvalues first 5',vals[:5]); print('variance explained first 5',rat[:5], 'cum2',rat[:2].sum())\\nfor name in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n j=features.index(name)\\n print(name,'PC1 loading/correlation',load[j,0],'PC2 loading/correlation',load[j,1],'PC1 eigenvector coefficient',vecs[j,0],'PC2 eigenvector coefficient',vecs[j,1])\\nprint('loadings all PC1/PC2')\\nprint(pd.DataFrame(load[:,:2],index=features,columns=['PC1','PC2']).round(4).to_string())\\nprint('scores by BCE ascending')\\nsc=pd.DataFrame(scores[:,:2],columns=['PC1_score','PC2_score']);sc['BCE']=df.BCE\\nprint(sc.sort_values('BCE')[['BCE','PC1_score','PC2_score']].round(4).to_string(index=False))\\nprint('PC2 score summary',sc.PC2_score.describe().round(4).to_dict())\\nprint('PC2 score range BCE',sc.loc[sc.PC2_score.idxmin(),'BCE'],sc.loc[sc.PC2_score.idxmax(),'BCE'])\\nPY\\npython /tmp/pca_capital.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 60}"

Original openness score through 'Belau_PC1', 100-year smoothed openness score, and linear interpolation of openness score have been used to signify growth. Demographic growth manifests itself in growth set bringing with it a further opening of the landscape. Opening of landscape corresponds to higher growth rates. During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape. The growth rate is defined as the percentage change of the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_grate_100). 7.
